Asymptotic stability of the equilibrium for the free boundary problem of a compressible atmospheric primitive model with physical vacuum
Xin Liu, Edriss S. Titi, and, Zhouping Xin

Abstract
This paper concerns the large time asymptotic behavior of solutions to the free boundary problem of the compressible primitive equations in atmospheric dynamics with physical vacuum. Up to second order of the perturbations of an equilibrium, we have introduced a model of the compressible primitive equations with a specific viscosity and shown that the physical vacuum free boundary problem for this model system has a global-in-time solution converging to an equilibrium exponentially, provided that the initial data is a small perturbation of the equilibrium. More precisely, we introduce a new coordinate system by choosing the enthalpy (the square of sound speed) as the vertical coordinate, and thanks to the hydrostatic balance, the degenerate density at the free boundary admits a representation with separation of variables in the new coordinates. Such a property allows us to establish horizontal derivative estimates without involving the singular vertical derivative of the density profile, which plays a key role in our analysis. Keywords: Hydrostatic approximations, Compressible flows, Asymptotic stability, Free boundary problem, Physical vacuum, Primitive equations, Modified viscosity, Atmospheric dynamics MSC2020: 35Q35, 76E20, 76N10, 86A10



1
Introduction

1.1
Compressible flows with physical vacuum

The dynamics of compressible flows is governed by the compressible Euler or Navier-Stokes system, describing the conservations of mass, momentum, and energy. See, e.g.,
[
13
,
12
,
33
]
. In particular, for an isentropic flow, under the influence of gravity, one has that

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\rho+\mathrm{div}\,(\rho u)=0,\\
\partial_{t}(\rho u)+\mathrm{div}\,(\rho u\otimes u)+\nabla P(\rho)=\mathcal{D}_{v}-\rho g\vec{e}_{3},\end{cases}

(CNS)

where
\rho\in[0,\infty),u\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
are the scalar and vector-valued functions, representing the density and the velocity field of the compressible flow, respectively. Here
P(\rho)=\rho^{\gamma}
is the pressure in isentropic flows, for
\gamma>1
.
\mathcal{D}_{v}
represents the viscous term in the Navier-Stokes equations, and
\vec{e}_{3}=(0,0,1)^{\top}
.
We consider the stationary equilibrium state of system (
CNS
),
(\rho_{e},u_{e})\equiv(\bar{\rho},0)
with
\bar{\rho}=\bar{\rho}(z)
satisfying

\nabla P(\bar{\rho})=-\bar{\rho}g\vec{e}_{3}.

Then it is clear that in the subset
\{\bar{\rho}>0\}
,

-\infty<\partial_{z}P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})=\gamma\partial_{z}\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}<0.

In particular, when vacuum occurs, i.e.,
\{\bar{\rho}=0\}\neq\emptyset
,
\bar{\rho}
is only Hölder continuous across the gas-vacuum interface.

In this work, the stationary density profile is taken to be

\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}=\dfrac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}g(1-z),

(1.1)

where
\{z=0\}
and
\{z=1\}
is the solid ground and the stationary gas-vacuum interface, respectively.

Such a kind of singularity/degeneracy is referred to as the physical vacuum and it appears widely in compressible flows connected to vacuum, e.g., the flows in porous media
[
1
,
34
]
and configurations of the gaseous stars
[
3
,
26
,
14
]
. We also refer to related works in
[
4
,
5
,
40
,
41
,
2
]
. Such a singularity makes the study of the evolutionary problem more challenging. As pointed out by T.-P. Liu in
[
34
]
, the standard symmetrizing method of hyperbolic systems does not apply in the case of compressible Euler equations with physical vacuum density. Essentially, on the gas-vacuum interface, the compressible Euler equations loses the hyperbolicity and
the characteristic surfaces hit the vacuum surface tangentially and then bounce back
. The local well-posedness of the free boundary problem with physical vacuum is developed in
[
22
,
23
,
20
,
43
,
16
,
17
,
8
,
7
,
6
]
.
In the case of one-dimensional motion or spherically symmetric motion, the long time dynamics has been investigated for the compressible Euler equations with damping
[
55
,
46
]
, the compressible Navier-Stokes equations
[
54
,
42
,
37
]
, and the gaseous star problem
[
18
,
21
,
24
,
45
,
44
,
35
,
36
]
. Without the symmetry assumption, see, e.g.,
[
19
,
49
,
50
]
for the study of the compressible Euler equations, and
[
56
,
57
,
58
]
for the study of the compressible Euler equations with damping.

On the other hand, the asymptotic stability of the physical vacuum profile (
1.1
) with general perturbation is still open, for either the compressible Euler or Navier-Stokes equations. The main difficulty is that any spatial tangential/horizontal derivative of the momentum equations
\eqref{CNS}_{2}
will involve derivative of
\rho
along the singular/gradient direction (
\nabla\rho
), regardless how small the perturbation is. In this paper, we consider a compressible model in the context of atmospheric dynamics, and establish the asymptotic stability result for the underlying equilibrium.

Specifically, we study system (
CNS
) with the
hydrostatic approximation
, which can be obtained by performing a small aspect ratio (between vertical and horizontal scales) limit.
The resulting equations are referred to as the compressible primitive equations (CPE) of atmospheric dynamics (c.f.
[
10
]
, or see (
FBCPE
), below). Similarly, as in (
CNS
), the physical vacuum profile
(\rho_{e},u_{e})=(\bar{\rho},0)
with
\bar{\rho}
as in (
1.1
) is a stationary solution to the CPE. We will investigate the nonlinear stability of such a profile and establish a global solution for the compressible flow with the physical vacuum singularity in multi-dimensional space without imposing symmetry.

The hydrostatic approximation, i.e., formally replacing the equation of the vertical momentum in (
CNS
) by the hydrostatic balance equation (or quasi-static equilibrium equation)

\partial_{z}P(\rho)=-\rho g,

(1.2)

is used to model flows with a much larger horizontal scale than the vertical scale such as the case of atmospheric flows. Indeed, the hydrostatic balance approximation turns out to be highly accurate for atmosphere and is a fundamental equation in the atmospheric science (see, e.g.,
[
32
,
48
,
53
]
).

The major stabilizing feature of the CPE in comparison to (
CNS
) is that, the hydrostatic balance (
1.2
) indicates that the density is always monotone in the vertical direction. This inspires us to introduce the change of coordinates (
1.6
), below, which is the key starting point of this work.

The first mathematical study of the CPE can be tracked back to Lions, Temam, and Wang
[
32
]
. By formulating the equations in the pressure coordinates (
p
-coordinates), the resulting equations are in the form of the incompressible primitive equations (referred to as the primitive equations, or the PE in the following). In yet another work
[
31
]
, the same authors established the PE as the hydrostatic approximation of the Boussinesq equations. They showed the existence of global weak solutions and therefore indirectly studied the CPE. See
[
29
,
30
]
for more works by the authors.
With fixed boundaries, Gatapov, Kazhikhov, Ersoy, and Ngom constructed a global weak solution to the two-dimensional CPE in
[
9
,
15
]
. The uniqueness of such weak solutions is shown in
[
25
]
. Meanwhile, Ersoy, Ngom, Sy, Tang, and Gao studied the compactness/stability of sequences of weak solutions to the CPE with degenerate viscosities in
[
10
,
51
]
.
In
[
38
]
, we show the existence of global weak solutions of the CPE with degenerate viscosities satisfying the entropy conditions. The same result is independently shown in
[
52
]
. Also, in
[
39
]
, we show the local well-posedness of strong solutions to the CPE.

1.2
The change of coordinates and a viscous compressible primitive system

This work is a contribution to our program of studying the compressible primitive equations. We focus here on investigating the long time dynamics of the CPE with the free boundary condition,
and the stability of the equilibrium state with physical vacuum.
The major benefit given by the hydrostatic approximation is that the enthalpy (the square of the sound speed)
\partial_{\rho}P(\rho)
is determined by the height of the moving boundary (
1.4
), below. Consequently, by taking the ratio between the enthalpy and its value on the solid ground as the vertical coordinate (see (
1.6
), below) together with the equation for the square of the sound speed and the new “vertical velocity” (
1.8
), below, we will arrive at a reformulation of the free boundary problem in a fixed domain (see (
1.9
), below). Such a formulation introduces a system that describes explicitly the physical vacuum in the degenerate direction, a separation of variables of the density, and a simple representation of the dynamical boundary condition (see (
1.10
), below). However, the loss of evolutionary equation for the vertical velocity, due to the hydrostatic balance, brings new degeneracy to the system. We overcome this difficulty through a complicated, but straightforwards “vertical velocity” formula (
1.18
), below. Moreover, as we will explain later, since we only focus on the global stability theory, we will
linearize the viscosities and the boundary condition
in our equations. This is done by neglecting the
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})
terms, with
\varepsilon
representing the size of perturbation. However, this disrupts the conservation of momentum. To avoid this shortcoming, we add some momentum correction term on the right-hand side of the momentum equations. Such a correction is important for establishing the Poincaré type inequality (Lemma
3
) and showing the asymptotic stability (Proposition
14
). In fact, we show that the perturbation around the equilibrium state will decay exponentially for
\gamma>4/3
, which incidentally is the same stability threshold established for the gaseous star problem (see
[
44
,
45
,
21
,
28
]
). We refer to such a system with the momentum correction term as the conservative system. In section
8
, we also investigate a non-conservative system with only the linear viscosity terms.

We remark that the aforementioned reformulation of the free boundary problem of the compressible primitive equations has been introduced and presented earlier in
[
39
]
. Below, we will recall it for the sake of completeness. Indeed, by denoting
v,w
as the horizontal and vertical components of the velocity
u
(i.e.
u=(v,w)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
), the compressible primitive system with moving free boundary is given by:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\rho+\mathrm{div}_{h}\,(\rho v)+\partial_{z}(\rho w)=0&\text{in}~\Omega(t),\\
\partial_{t}(\rho v)+\mathrm{div}_{h}\,(\rho v\otimes v)+\partial_{z}(\rho wv)+\nabla_{h}P=\mathcal{D}_{v}&\text{in}~\Omega(t),\\
\partial_{z}P+\rho g=0&\text{in}~\Omega(t),\end{cases}

(FBCPE)

where
P=\rho^{\gamma},\gamma>1
and
\mathcal{D}_{v}=\mathcal{D}_{v}(\nabla v)
is the eddy viscosity term given by

\mathcal{D}_{v}:=\mu\mathrm{div}_{h}\,(\nabla_{h}v+\nabla_{h}v^{\top})+\lambda\nabla_{h}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v+\mu^{\prime}\partial_{zz}v,

with the constant viscosity coefficients
\mu,\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}
satisfying the Lamé conditions
\mu>0,\mu^{\prime}>0,\mu+\lambda>0
.
Here
\Omega(t)=\{\vec{x}=(\vec{x}_{h},z)\in\mathbb{T}^{2}\times(0,Z(\vec{x}_{h},t))\}
is the evolving domain, with
z=0
being the bottom solid ground and
z=Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)
being the interface (free boundary) connecting the flows and the vacuum.

System (
FBCPE
) should be complemented with
the following boundary conditions:

\begin{gathered}-\partial_{t}Z-v|_{z=Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z+w|_{z=Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)}=0,\\
P|_{z=Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)}=0,w|_{z=0}=0,\partial_{z}v|_{z=0}=0,\\
\biggl(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mu(\nabla_{h}v+\nabla_{h}v^{\top})+\lambda\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\mathbb{I}_{2}&\mu^{\prime}\partial_{z}v\\
0&0\end{array}\biggr)\Big|_{z=Z}\vec{N}(\vec{x}_{h},t)=0,\\
\text{where}~\vec{N}(\vec{x}_{h},t)=\biggl(\begin{array}[]{c}-\nabla_{h}Z\\
1\end{array}\biggr).\end{gathered}

(1.3)

In this work, we assume that
\mu=\mu^{\prime}>0
and
\lambda>0
.

The hydrostatic balance equation
\eqref{isen-CPE-fb}_{3}
yields that the enthalpy (the square of sound speed) can be calculated as

\displaystyle\rho^{\gamma-1}(\vec{x}_{h},z,t)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}g(Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)-z)+\rho^{\gamma-1}(\vec{x}_{h},Z(\vec{x}_{h},t),t)

(1.4)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}g(Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)-z),

where we use in the above that
\gamma>1
and that the density at the free boundary
z=Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)
vanishes.
On the other hand, from
\eqref{isen-CPE-fb}_{1}
, we have

\partial_{t}\rho^{\gamma-1}+v\cdot\nabla_{h}\rho^{\gamma-1}+w\partial_{z}\rho^{\gamma-1}+(\gamma-1)\rho^{\gamma-1}(\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v+\partial_{z}w)=0.

(1.5)

Now, we define the new coordinates as

\begin{gathered}\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime}=(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}):=\vec{x}_{h}=(x,y),~t^{\prime}=t,\\
z^{\prime}:=\dfrac{\rho^{\gamma-1}}{\rho^{\gamma-1}|_{z=0}}=\dfrac{Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)-z}{Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)}=1-\dfrac{z}{Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)}.\end{gathered}

(1.6)

In this new coordinate system,
\{z^{\prime}=0\}
and
\{z^{\prime}=1\}
correspond to the upper moving boundary connecting the fluid to the vacuum and the bottom fixed boundary, respectively.
It is easy to verify that
\nabla_{\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime}}Z=\nabla_{\vec{x}_{h}}Z,\partial_{t}Z=\partial_{t^{\prime}}Z
and the following chain rules:

\displaystyle\partial_{x}

\displaystyle=\partial_{x^{\prime}}+\biggl(\dfrac{z}{Z^{2}}\partial_{x}Z\biggr)\partial_{z^{\prime}}=\partial_{x^{\prime}}+\biggl(\dfrac{1-z^{\prime}}{Z}\partial_{x^{\prime}}Z\biggr)\partial_{z^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\partial_{y}

\displaystyle=\partial_{y^{\prime}}+\biggl(\dfrac{z}{Z^{2}}\partial_{y}Z\biggr)\partial_{z^{\prime}}=\partial_{y^{\prime}}+\biggl(\dfrac{1-z^{\prime}}{Z}\partial_{y^{\prime}}Z\biggr)\partial_{z^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{Z}\partial_{z^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t^{\prime}}+\biggl(\dfrac{z}{Z^{2}}\partial_{t}Z\biggr)\partial_{z^{\prime}}=\partial_{t^{\prime}}+\biggl(\dfrac{1-z^{\prime}}{Z}\partial_{t^{\prime}}Z\biggr)\partial_{z^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\vec{x}^{\prime}=(\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\in\Omega_{h}\times(0,1)=:\Omega^{\prime}_{e}.

Recall that
\Omega_{h}=\mathbb{T}^{2}
is the horizontal domain.
Now we rewrite equations (
FBCPE
) and (
1.5
) in the new coordinates
(\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime},z^{\prime},t^{\prime})
as follows:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t^{\prime}}Z+v\cdot\nabla_{\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime}}Z-w+(\gamma-1)z^{\prime}\bigl(Z\mathrm{div}\,_{\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime}}v\\
~~~~~~~~~~~~-(z^{\prime}-1)\nabla_{\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime}}Z\cdot\partial_{z^{\prime}}v-\partial_{z^{\prime}}w\bigr)=0&\text{in}~\Omega^{\prime}_{e},\\
\rho\bigl(\partial_{t^{\prime}}v+v\cdot\nabla_{\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime}}v+\dfrac{1}{Z}(-(z^{\prime}-1)\partial_{t^{\prime}}Z-(z^{\prime}-1)v\cdot\nabla_{\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime}}Z\\
~~~~~~~~~~~~-w)\partial_{z^{\prime}}v+g\nabla_{\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime}}Z\bigr)=\mathcal{D}_{v}^{\prime}&\text{in}~\Omega^{\prime}_{e},\\
\partial_{z^{\prime}}Z=0&\text{in}~\Omega^{\prime}_{e},\end{cases}

where

\rho=\biggl(\dfrac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}gz^{\prime}Z\biggr)^{1/(\gamma-1)},

(1.7)

and
\mathcal{D}_{v}^{\prime}
represents the viscosity
\mathcal{D}_{v}
in the
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime},z^{\prime},t^{\prime})
-coordinates.

Moreover, we define the new “vertical velocity”

W(\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime},z^{\prime},t^{\prime}):=\dfrac{-(z^{\prime}-1)\partial_{t^{\prime}}Z-(z^{\prime}-1)v\cdot\nabla_{\vec{x}_{h}^{\prime}}Z-w}{Z}.

(1.8)

Then dropping the prime sign yields the following system

\begin{cases}\gamma z(\partial_{t}Z+v\cdot\nabla_{h}Z)+(\gamma-1)zZ(\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v+\partial_{z}W)+ZW=0&\text{in}~\Omega_{e},\\
\rho(\partial_{t}v+v\cdot\nabla_{h}v+W\partial_{z}v+g\nabla_{h}Z)=\mathcal{D}_{v}&\text{in}~\Omega_{e},\\
\partial_{z}Z=0&\text{in}~\Omega_{e},\end{cases}

(1.9)

with
\Omega_{e}=\Omega_{h}\times(0,1)=\mathbb{T}^{2}\times(0,1)
and
\rho=\bigl(\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}gzZ\bigr)^{1/(\gamma-1)}
given by (
1.7
). Meanwhile, from the definitions in (
1.6
) and (
1.8
), the boundary conditions in (
1.3
) yield

W|_{z=0,1}=0.

(1.10)

We recall that
Z
is the height of the free boundary and is also proportional to the enthalpy on the ground, from which the pressure (potential) on the ground can be recovered as

P_{s}=\bigl(\dfrac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}gZ\bigr)^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}.

W
serves as if it is the new “vertical velocity” in this formulation.
The equilibrium state
(\rho_{e},u_{e})=(\bar{\rho},0)
is represented by

(Z_{e},v_{e},W_{e})\equiv(1,0,0)\qquad\text{or}\qquad(Z_{e},v_{e})\equiv(1,0).

(1.11)

Next, multiplying
\eqref{rfeq:isen-CPE-fb}_{1}
with
z^{1/(\gamma-1)-1}
leads to

\gamma z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(\partial_{t}Z+v\cdot\nabla_{h}Z)+(\gamma-1)z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,vZ+(\gamma-1)Z\partial_{z}(z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}W)=0.

(1.12)

Noticing that since
\partial_{z}Z=0
, integrating the above equation with respect to the vertical variable
Z
over the interval
(0,1)
and using (
1.10
) yields

(\gamma-1)\partial_{t}Z+\gamma\biggl(\overline{z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}v}\biggr)\cdot\nabla_{h}Z+(\gamma-1)\biggl(\overline{z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}\biggr)Z=0,

(1.13)

where we have used and will use the notation

\overline{f}:=\int_{0}^{1}f(z)\,dz.

(1.14)

Next, we eliminate
\partial_{t}Z
by employing (
1.12
) and (
1.13
) to obtain

\displaystyle(\gamma-1)Z\partial_{z}(z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}W)=\dfrac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\bigl[\gamma\bigl(\overline{z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}v}\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{h}Z+(\gamma-1)\bigl(\overline{z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}\bigr)Z\bigr]

\displaystyle~~~~-\gamma z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}v\cdot\nabla_{h}Z-(\gamma-1)z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\bigl(\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr)Z.

Therefore, we have the following representation of
W
, after applying the fundamental theorem of calculus in the
z
-variable and using (
1.10
),

\displaystyle(\gamma-1)z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}ZW=z^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}\bigl[\gamma\bigl(\overline{z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}v}\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{h}Z+(\gamma-1)\bigl(\overline{z^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}\bigr)Z\bigr]

(1.15)

\displaystyle-\gamma\biggl(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}v(\vec{x}_{h},\xi,t)\,d\xi\biggr)\cdot\nabla_{h}Z

\displaystyle-(\gamma-1)Z\biggl(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v(\vec{x}_{h},\xi,t)\,d\xi\biggr).

Hence, the above relation recovers the “vertical velocity”.

Next, we make a comment on the derivation of (
1.9
). In
[
53
, Chapter 3]
, the
\sigma
-coordinate system is introduced to reformulate (
FBCPE
). Our choice of coordinates (
1.6
) shares the same philosophy as the
\sigma
-coordinates as well as the
p
-coordinates in
[
32
]
.
Denote by
\alpha:=\frac{1}{\gamma-1}
. On the other hand, the viscosity
\mathcal{D}_{v}
becomes very complicated in the new coordinates and brings additional difficulty to our analysis. To avoid this, we consider a model whose viscosity is derived by linearizing the
\mathcal{D}_{v}
and taking into account the momentum correction term (see Remark
2
, below).
Then system (
1.9
) can be modified as

\begin{cases}(\alpha+1)z(\partial_{t}Z+v\cdot\nabla_{h}Z)+zZ(\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v+\partial_{z}W)\\
~~~~~~~~+\alpha ZW=0&\text{in}~\Omega_{e},\\
\rho(\partial_{t}v+v\cdot\nabla_{h}v+W\partial_{z}v+g\nabla_{h}Z)=\mu\Delta_{h}v+(\mu+\lambda)\nabla_{h}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\\
~~~~~~~~+\mu\partial_{zz}v+\mu\bigl(\nabla_{h}\log Z\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{h}v+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl(\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr)\nabla_{h}\log Z&\text{in}~\Omega_{e},\\
\partial_{z}Z=0&\text{in}~\Omega_{e},\end{cases}

(1.16)

with (
1.7
), (
1.13
), and (
1.15
) being written as

\displaystyle\Omega_{e}=\Omega_{h}\times(0,1),~~\rho=\biggl(\dfrac{1}{\alpha+1}gzZ\biggr)^{\alpha}=:A_{\alpha}z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha},{}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Z+(\alpha+1)\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z+\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}Z=0,

(1.17)

\displaystyle z^{\alpha}ZW=z^{\alpha+1}\bigl((\alpha+1)\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z+\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}Z\bigr)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~-(\alpha+1)\biggl(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v\,d\xi\biggr)\cdot\nabla_{h}Z-\biggl(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\xi\biggr)\cdot Z,

(1.18)

where the viscosity has the form

\begin{gathered}\underbrace{\mu\Delta_{h}v+(\mu+\lambda)\nabla_{h}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v+\mu\partial_{zz}v}_{\text{linearized viscosity}}\\
+\underbrace{\mu\bigl(\nabla_{h}\log Z\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{h}v+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl(\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr)\nabla_{h}\log Z}_{\text{momentum correction}}.\end{gathered}

(1.19)

The following boundary conditions for (
1.16
) are imposed:

W|_{z=0,1}=0,\qquad\partial_{z}v|_{z=0,1}=0.

(1.20)

Without loss of generality, we assume
A_{\alpha}\equiv 1
. We will study (
1.16
) with initial data
(Z,v)|_{t=0}=(Z_{0},v_{0})\in H^{2}(\Omega_{e})\times H^{2}(\Omega_{e})
satisfying

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{e}}\rho vZ\,d\vec{x}\big|_{t=0}=\int_{\Omega_{e}}\bigl(z^{\alpha}Z_{0}^{\alpha}v_{0}Z_{0}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}=0,

(1.21)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{e}}Z^{\alpha+1}\,d\vec{x}\big|_{t=0}=\int_{\Omega_{e}}Z_{0}^{\alpha+1}\,d\vec{x}=1.

Remark 1
.

Following the derivation of (
1.9
), the ‘real’ physical viscosity term and the boundary conditions should be given by applying the change of coordinates to the momentum equations in (
FBCPE
) and (
1.3
). In particular, the viscosity
\mathcal{D}_{v}
in the new coordinates is much more complicated than the viscosity (
1.19
). However, we aim at studying the stability of the steady state given by
(Z_{e},v_{e})\equiv(1,0)
. Therefore, the change of coordinates will only bring some terms which are at least quadratic and
o(1)
as the perturbation size goes to zero, although these terms may involve higher order derivatives. For this reason, we study here the modified viscosity and boundary conditions as a plausible model, which will substantially simplify the calculations and presentation. The original case will be treated in future study.

Remark 2
.

The momentum correction term

\mu\bigl(\nabla_{h}\log Z\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{h}v+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl(\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr)\nabla_{h}\log Z,

is used to recover the physical principle of the conservation of momentum. In fact, since we have simplified the viscosity term and the boundary conditions by dropping the
o(1)
terms, the conservative structure of the momentum is violated. We fix this by putting this correction term at the right-hand side of
\eqref{FB-CPE}_{2}
and we will have the conservation of momentum,

\dfrac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\rho vZ\,d\vec{x}=0.

Also, from (
1.17
) we have the conservation of mass,

\dfrac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega_{h}}Z^{\alpha+1}\,d\vec{x}_{h}=0.

The rest of this work will be organized as follows. In the next section, we introduce basic notations and frequently used inequalities. Furthermore, we will also state our main theorems. In section
3
, we show the global
a priori
estimates, which together with the local well-posedness theory in section
5.1
, show the global well-posedness and stability in section
5
. In section
4
and section
7
, we will explore the regularity of the solutions. This will finish the proof of Theorem
1
, below. The asymptotic stability theory is established in section
6
, which is summarized as Theorem
2
, below. In section
8
, we introduce and show the global stability theory for a non-conservative system.

2
Preliminaries and main theorems

In section
2.1
, below, we introduce some notations and inequalities that will be used in this paper. With the energy and dissipation functionals defined in section
2.2
, we will state the main theorems in section
2.3
.

2.1
Notations and Hardy’s inequality

As we stated before, we will use the following notations to denote the differential operators in the horizontal directions:

\displaystyle\nabla_{h}:=(\partial_{x},\partial_{y})^{\top},\qquad\partial_{h}\in\{\partial_{x},\partial_{y}\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{h}\,:=\nabla_{h}\cdot,\qquad\Delta_{h}:=\mathrm{div}_{h}\,\nabla_{h}.

We will use
\bigl|\cdot\bigr|,\bigl\Arrowvert\cdot\bigr\Arrowvert
to denote norms in
\Omega_{h}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
, respectively.
Correspondingly, the associated Sobolev norm in the horizontal variables is denoted as,

\bigl\Arrowvert\cdot\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{s}_{h}}:=\bigl\Arrowvert\cdot\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}\cdot\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\cdots+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}^{s}\cdot\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},~\text{for $s\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}$}.

For instance, given
f\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R})=L^{2}(\Omega_{h}\times(0,1);\mathbb{R})
, denote by

\displaystyle\bigl|f\bigr|_{L^{2}}(z):=\bigl(\int_{\Omega_{h}}\bigl|f(\vec{x}_{h},z)\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}_{h}\bigr)^{1/2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bigl\Arrowvert f\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}:=\bigl(\int\bigl|f\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\bigr)^{1/2},

where

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{h}}\cdot\,d\vec{x}_{h}=\int_{\Omega_{h}}\cdot\,dxdy\qquad\text{and}\qquad\int\cdot\,d\vec{x}=\int_{\Omega}\cdot\,d\vec{x}=\int_{\Omega}\cdot\,dxdydz,

represent the integrals with respect to the horizontal variables and the full spatial variables, respectively. Then, with the notation defined in (
1.14
), it is easy to verify,

\displaystyle\bigl|\overline{f}\bigr|_{H^{s}}\leq C\bigl\Arrowvert f\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{s}_{h}},

for some constant
0<C<\infty
provided the right-hand side is finite. In addition, we have the following property:

Lemma 1
.

Consider
p_{i}\geq 1,\alpha_{i}>0
satisfying

\dfrac{\alpha_{1}}{p_{1}}+\dfrac{\alpha_{2}}{p_{2}}+\cdots\dfrac{\alpha_{n}}{p_{n}}\leq 1.

The following Hölder’s inequality holds:

\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|f_{1}\bigr|_{L^{p_{1}}}^{\alpha_{1}}\bigl|f_{2}\bigr|_{L^{p_{2}}}^{\alpha_{2}}\cdots\bigl|f_{n}\bigr|_{L^{p_{n}}}^{\alpha_{n}}\,dz\leq C\bigl\Arrowvert f_{1}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{p_{1}}}^{\alpha_{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert f_{2}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{p_{2}}}^{\alpha_{2}}\cdots\bigl\Arrowvert f_{n}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{p_{n}}}^{\alpha_{n}},

provided the right-hand side is finite.

After applying the
Gagliardo-Nirenberg (or Ladyzhenskaya) inequality
in
\Omega_{h}
(c.f.
[
47
]
), one has

\begin{gathered}\bigl|f\bigr|_{L^{4}}\leq C\bigl|f\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\bigl|f\bigr|_{H^{1}}^{1/2},\\
\bigl|f\bigr|_{\infty}\leq C\bigl|f\bigr|_{W^{1,4}}\leq C\bigl|f\bigr|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\bigl|f\bigr|_{H^{2}}^{1/2},\end{gathered}

(2.1)

for the function
f
with bounded right-hand sides.

The standard Hardy inequalities read:

Lemma 2

(Hardy’s inequalities)
.

Let
k
and
L
be given real and positive numbers, respectively, and let
g:[0,L]\mapsto\mathbb{R}
be a measurable function satisfying
\int_{0}^{L}s^{k}({\bigl|g(s)\bigr|^{2}}/{L^{2}}+\bigl|g^{\prime}(s)\bigr|^{2})\,ds<\infty
.

a)

If
k>1
, then we have

\int_{0}^{L}s^{k-2}\bigl|g(s)\bigr|^{2}\,ds\leq C\int_{0}^{L}s^{k}(\bigl|g(s)\bigr|^{2}/L^{2}+\bigl|g^{\prime}(s)\bigr|^{2})\,ds;

b)

if
k<1
, then
g
has a trace at
x=0
and, moreover,

\int_{0}^{L}s^{k-2}\bigl|g(s)-g(0)\bigr|^{2}\,ds\leq C\int_{0}^{L}s^{k}\bigl|g^{\prime}(s)\bigr|^{2}\,ds;

c)

if
k>1
, then we have, for
0<\omega<1
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\omega}s^{k-2}\bigl|g(s)\bigr|^{2}\,ds\leq C\omega^{-2}\int_{0}^{\omega}s^{k}\bigl|g(s)\bigr|^{2}\,ds+C\omega^{2}\int_{0}^{\omega}s^{k-2}\bigl|g^{\prime}(s)\bigr|^{2}\,ds,

provided the right-hand side is finite.

Here
0<C<\infty
is a positive constant independent of
L
and
\omega
.

Proof.

We refer
a),b)
to
[
21
]
and
[
27
]
.
c)
is a special case of
a)
.
∎

A straight forward calculation shows that Hardy’s inequalities in Lemma
2
hold with
s
replaced by
z
and
\bigl|g(s)\bigr|,\bigl|g^{\prime}(s)\bigr|
replaced by
\bigl|f\bigr|_{L^{2}}(z),\bigl|\partial_{z}f\bigr|_{L^{2}}(z)
, respectively. Additionally, we will apply the special case when
L=1
throughout this paper.

For any two quantities
A,B
, we use
A\lesssim B
to denote that there is a scale invariant constant
0<C<\infty
such that

A\leq CB.

We use the notation

\mathcal{H}(\cdot)

to represent a polynomial of its arguments, which may be different from line to line, satisfying

\mathcal{H}(0)=0.

2.2
Energy and dissipation functionals

In this section, we introduce the main functionals to be used in this work.
Recalling that
\alpha=\frac{1}{\gamma-1}
, we introduce first, the following energy functionals,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t):=\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{z}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}

(2.2)

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert Z-1\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, we denote the total initial energy as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{0}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{1}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{0,z}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert v_{0}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}

(2.3)

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{0}-1\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{1}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2},

where
v_{0}=v|_{t=0},v_{1}=v_{t}|_{t=0},Z_{1}=Z_{t}|_{t=0}
are given by

\displaystyle z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}v_{1}=\mu\Delta_{h}v_{0}+(\mu+\lambda)\nabla_{h}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{0}+\mu\partial_{zz}v_{0}+\mu\nabla_{h}\log Z_{0}\cdot\nabla_{h}v_{0}

\displaystyle~~~~+(\mu+\lambda)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{0}\nabla_{h}\log Z_{0}-gz^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}_{0}\nabla_{h}Z_{0}-z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}v_{0}\cdot\nabla_{h}v_{0}

\displaystyle~~~~-z^{\alpha+1}\bigl(\dfrac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{0}}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z_{0}^{\alpha}+\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{0}}Z_{0}^{\alpha}\bigr)\partial_{z}v_{0}

\displaystyle~~~~+\dfrac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v_{0}\,d\xi\cdot\nabla_{h}Z_{0}^{\alpha}\partial_{z}v_{0}+\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{0}\,d\xi Z_{0}^{\alpha}\partial_{z}v_{0},

\displaystyle Z_{1}=-(\alpha+1)\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{0}}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z_{0}-\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{0}}Z_{0}.

For some constant
0<c<\infty
, we denote the total relative energy as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{c}(t):=\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{\alpha+2}\int\biggl(\bigl(Z^{\alpha+2}-1\bigr)-\dfrac{\alpha+2}{\alpha+1}\bigl(Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr)\biggr)\,d\vec{x}

(2.4)

\displaystyle+c\int\biggl(\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu+\lambda}{2}\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle-\dfrac{g}{\alpha+1}z^{\alpha}\bigl(Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\biggr)\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{1}{2}\int\biggl(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl(\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle+\bigl|\nabla_{h}^{2}v\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|v_{z}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{z}\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\biggr)\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{2}\int\biggl(\bigl(Z^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}+Z^{\alpha}\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle+\bigl|\nabla_{h}^{2}Z\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\biggr)\,d\vec{x}.

We observe that if
\mathcal{E}(t)
is sufficiently small,
\mathfrak{E}_{c}(t)
is equivalent to
\mathcal{E}(t)
for some fixed constant
c>0
:

Proposition 1
.

There is a constant
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
small enough, such that if
\mathcal{E}(t)<\varepsilon_{1}
for
t\in[0,T]
, the following inequalities hold for some positive constant
C<\infty
, depending on
\varepsilon_{1}
and independent of
t
,

\displaystyle\bigl|Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)-1\bigr|\leq C\varepsilon_{1}^{1/2}<1/2,

(2.5)

\displaystyle C^{-1}\mathcal{E}(t)\leq\mathfrak{E}_{c}(t)\leq C\mathcal{E}(t),

(2.6)

where

c:=\dfrac{\mu}{8g},

in equation (
2.4
).

Proof.

Notice first that,
C\mathcal{E}(t)\geq\bigl\Arrowvert Z(\cdot,t)-1\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{\infty}}^{2}
, which implies (
2.5
). Furthermore, for
\varepsilon_{1}
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\dfrac{\alpha+2}{4(\alpha+1)^{2}}\bigl|Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr|^{2}\leq(Z^{\alpha+2}-1)-\dfrac{\alpha+2}{\alpha+1}(Z^{\alpha+1}-1)

(
3.11
)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\alpha+2}{(\alpha+1)^{2}}\bigl|Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr|^{2}.

Meanwhile, after applying Young’s inequality, one has

\displaystyle\bigl|\dfrac{g}{\alpha+1}z^{\alpha}\bigl(Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr|\leq\dfrac{\mu}{4}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{g^{2}}{\mu(\alpha+1)^{2}}\bigl|Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr|^{2}.

Hence for
0<c\leq\frac{\mu}{8g}
, one has

\displaystyle C\mathcal{E}(t)\geq\mathfrak{E}_{c}(t)\geq\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{8(\alpha+1)^{2}}\int\bigl|Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~+c\int\dfrac{\mu}{4}\bigl|\nabla v\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{1}{2}\int\biggl(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl(\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~+\bigl|\nabla_{h}^{2}v\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|v_{z}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{z}\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\biggr)\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{2}\int\biggl(\bigl(Z^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}+Z^{\alpha}\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~+\bigl|\nabla_{h}^{2}Z\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\biggr)\,d\vec{x}\geq C^{-1}\mathcal{E}(t),

for some positive constant
1<C<\infty
. Here we have applied Hardy’s inequality, Lemma
2
, and, thanks to (
2.5
), the fact that
\bigl|Z-1\bigr|<1/2
as follows,

\displaystyle\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|v\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dz\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}z^{2}(\bigl|v\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2})\,dz\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}z^{4}\bigl|v\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~+\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dz\underbrace{\lesssim\cdots\lesssim}_{\max\{[\alpha/2]-1,0\}\text{ times}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2},

(2.7)

\displaystyle\bigl|Z-1\bigr|=\bigl|(Z^{\alpha+1})^{1/(\alpha+1)}-1\bigr|\lesssim\bigl|Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr|\lesssim\bigl|Z-1\bigr|.

(2.8)

This finishes the proof.
∎

In addition, we introduce the relative dissipation functional,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}(t):=\dfrac{\mu}{2}\int Z\bigl(\bigl|\nabla v\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\nabla v_{t}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\nabla^{2}v\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\nabla^{2}\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}

(2.9)

\displaystyle+\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\int\bigl(\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|^{2}+\bigl|\nabla_{h}^{2}Z\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}.

We have the following lemma:

Proposition 2
.

In addition to the assumptions in Proposition
1
, suppose that
(Z,v)
is a solution to (
1.16
). Then there is a constant
0<C<\infty
such that,

\displaystyle C^{-1}\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert Z-1\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}

(2.10)

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigr)\leq\mathfrak{D}(t)\leq C\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert Z-1\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigr).

Proof.

As the consequence of (
2.8
) and (
2.9
), it suffices to show

\bigl\Arrowvert Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}.

But this is a direct consequence of the conservation of mass
\int Z^{\alpha+1}\,d\vec{x}=\int Z_{0}^{\alpha+1}\,d\vec{x}=1
(see (
3.3
) in Proposition
4
, below) , the Poincaré inequality, and (
2.5
).
∎

2.3
Main theorems

Now we can state the main theorems in this contribution.

Theorem 1

(Global Stability)
.

Recalling
\alpha=\frac{1}{\gamma-1}
, consider initial data
(Z,v)|_{t=0}=(Z_{0},v_{0})\in H^{2}(\Omega)\times H^{2}(\Omega)
satisfying the compatible conditions,

\displaystyle\partial_{z}v_{0}|_{z=0,1}=0,~\partial_{z}Z_{0}=0,~\int Z_{0}^{\alpha+1}\,d\vec{x}=1,~W_{0}|_{z=0,1}=0,

where
W_{0}
is defined in (
1.18
) by replacing
v
and
Z
with
v_{0}
and
Z_{0}
, respectively.
Suppose that
\mathcal{E}_{0}<\varepsilon^{\prime}
for some positive constant
\varepsilon^{\prime}
given in Proposition
13
, below. Then:

I.

There is a unique strong global solution
(Z,v)
to the simplified model equations (
1.16
), subject to the boundary condition (
1.20
), for the free boundary problem of the compressible primitive equations. The global strong solution admits the following regularity:

\displaystyle Z-1\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;H^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Z\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle z^{\alpha/2}v,z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v,z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}^{2}v,z^{\alpha/2}\partial_{z}v,z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}\partial_{z}v\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle v\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{2}(\Omega)),\nabla v\in L^{2}(0,\infty;H^{1}(\Omega)),\nabla_{h}v\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle z^{\alpha/2}\partial_{t}v\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega)),\nabla v_{t}\in L^{2}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle z^{1/2}\partial_{zzz}v\in L^{2}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega)).

Moreover, for every
T\in(0,\infty)
, the following estimates hold for the solution
(Z,v)
,

\displaystyle\bullet\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}(t)+\int_{0}^{T}\biggl(\bigl\Arrowvert Z-1\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\biggr)\,dt\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0};

\displaystyle\bullet\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigl\Arrowvert v(t)\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}\leq C\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{0}+1\bigr)\mathcal{E}_{0}^{1/2};

\displaystyle\bullet\text{if $\alpha>1/2$, i.e., }1<\gamma<3,~\int_{0}^{T}\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{zzz}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}+C\mathcal{E}_{0}^{3};

\displaystyle\bullet\text{if $0<\alpha\leq 1/2$, i.e., }\gamma\geq 3,~\int_{0}^{T}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\beta}\partial_{zzz}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}+C\mathcal{E}_{0}^{3},

\displaystyle~~~~\text{with $\beta>\frac{1}{2}-\alpha$},

for some constant
0<C<\infty
. In particular,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{1/2}\partial_{zzz}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}+C\mathcal{E}_{0}^{3}.

II.

If in addition,
(Z_{0},v_{0})
satisfies

\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{0}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{3}}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}^{3}v_{0}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}<\infty,

(2.11)

then
for any
T\in(0,\infty)
, there is a positive constant
0<C_{T}<\infty
such that,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigl\{\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}^{3}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}^{3}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{t}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\bigr\}

\displaystyle~~~~+\int_{0}^{T}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla\nabla_{h}^{3}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\leq C_{T}\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}^{3}v_{0}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}^{3}Z_{0}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+1\bigr).

Proof of Theorem
1
.

The existence and uniqueness of the strong solutions follow from Proposition
13
, below. The estimates and regularity are given in Proposition
3
, Proposition
10
, and Proposition
11
, below. The estimate with initial data satisfying (
2.11
) is given in Proposition
15
, below.
∎

Theorem 2

(Asymptotic Stability)
.

Consider
(Z_{0},v_{0})\in H^{2}(\Omega)\times H^{2}(\Omega)
.
Suppose that in addition to the conditions given in Theorem
1
,
0<\alpha<3
(i.e.,
\gamma>\frac{4}{3}
) and (
1.21
) holds for the initial data, i.e.,

\int\bigl(z^{\alpha}Z_{0}^{\alpha+1}v_{0}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}=0.

(
1.21
)

Then, there exist constants
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<\varepsilon^{\prime},0<C_{1},C_{2}<\infty
, such that if
\mathcal{E}_{0}<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, we have the following decay estimate: for all
t>0
,

\mathcal{E}(t)\leq e^{-C_{1}t}C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{0}.

In particular,

\bigl|Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)-1\bigr|+\bigl|v(\vec{x},t)\bigr|\leq\bigl\Arrowvert Z(t)-1\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert v(t)\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}\leq Ce^{-Ct},

for some constant
0<C<\infty
.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

This is the consequence of Proposition
14
and Proposition
10
, below.
∎

Remark 3
.

The condition (
1.21
) is only required in showing the asymptotic stability, which can be reached via a Galilean transformation. The study of a non-conservative system, in which case such an assumption does not make sense, is discussed in section
8
.

With more regular initial data satisfying (
2.11
), Theorem
1
shows that the solution is more regular in the tangential direction. In particular, this will yield the change of coordinates (
1.6
) is regular, and the reformulated equations (
1.9
) are equivalent to the original equations (
FBCPE
).

3
A priori
estimates

In this section, we derive the required global
a priori
estimates which guarantee the stability of the equilibrium. We assume that the strong solution
(Z,v)
to (
1.16
) exists in
\Omega\times(0,T)
for some positive time
T\in(0,\infty)
and that
(Z,v)
is regular enough so that the quantities in the following relevant statements are finite and well-defined. This is the essential block of the continuity arguments later in this paper.
Indeed, we will establish the following proposition:

Proposition 3
.

Suppose that
(Z,v)
is a strong solution to (
1.16
) subject to the boundary condition (
1.20
).
There is a constant
\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,\varepsilon_{1})
, with
\varepsilon_{1}
given in Proposition
1
, such that if
\mathcal{E}(t)<\varepsilon_{2}
, for
t\in(0,T)
, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\sup_{0<t<T}\mathcal{E}(t)+\int_{0}^{T}\biggl(\bigl\Arrowvert Z-1\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}

(3.1)

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\biggr)\,dt\leq C_{1}\mathcal{E}_{0},

where
0<C_{1}<\infty
is a constant depending only on
\varepsilon_{2}
.

Throughout the rest of this section, without loss of generality,
we assume that

\dfrac{1}{2}<Z(\vec{x}_{h},t)<2,

(3.2)

for all
\vec{x}_{h}\in\Omega_{h}
and
t\in[0,T]
.

3.1
Basic energy

In this section, we will perform some
L^{2}
-energy estimates on (
1.16
). Our first lemma is concerning the basic energy of the system:

Proposition 4
.

Let
(Z,v)
be a regular enough strong solution on
[0,T]
to (
1.16
). Then one has the following, for all
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\bullet\text{Conservation of mass:}~~~~\int Z^{\alpha+1}(\vec{x}_{h},t)\,d\vec{x}=\int Z_{0}^{\alpha+1}(\vec{x}_{h},t)\,d\vec{x};

(3.3)

\displaystyle\bullet\text{Conservation of energy:}

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}(\vec{x}_{h},t)\bigl|v(\vec{x},t)\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{\alpha+2}\int\biggl(\bigl(Z^{\alpha+2}(\vec{x}_{h},t)-1\bigr)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~-\dfrac{\alpha+2}{\alpha+1}\bigl(Z^{\alpha+1}(\vec{x}_{h},t)-1\bigr)\biggr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~+\int_{0}^{T}\int Z\bigl(\mu\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr|^{2}+\mu\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}\,dt

(3.4)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z_{0}^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v_{0}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{\alpha+2}\int\biggl(\bigl(Z_{0}^{\alpha+2}-1\bigr)-\dfrac{\alpha+2}{\alpha+1}\bigl(Z_{0}^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr)\biggr)\,d\vec{x}.

Furthermore, the following inequalities hold for some constant
0<C<\infty
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~+C\int_{0}^{T}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}^{2}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}\,dt,

(3.5)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{3}}^{3}\,dt\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}^{3}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{3}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}^{3}\bigr)\,dt.

(3.6)

Proof.

Taking the
L^{2}
-inner produce of
\eqref{FB-CPE}_{2}
with
Zv
yields, after applying integration by parts in the resultant,

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}+\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}g\nabla_{h}Z\cdot v\,d\vec{x}

(3.7)

\displaystyle+\int Z\bigl[\mu\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr|^{2}+\mu\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|^{2}\bigr]\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=0,

where we have applied the fact that from
\eqref{FB-CPE}_{1}
, one has

\partial_{t}(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1})+\mathrm{div}_{h}\,(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}v)+\partial_{z}(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}W)=0.

(3.8)

On the other hand, after applying integration by parts and (
3.8
), one will have

\displaystyle\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}g\nabla_{h}Z\cdot v\,d\vec{x}=-g\int Z\mathrm{div}_{h}\,(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}v)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=g\int\bigl[\partial_{t}(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1})Z+\partial_{z}(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}W)Z\bigr]\,d\vec{x}=\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{(\alpha+1)g}{\alpha+2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{g}{\alpha+2}\int Z^{\alpha+2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\},

thanks to the fact that
\partial_{z}Z=0
.
In addition, integrating (
1.17
) leads to,

\dfrac{d}{dt}\int Z^{\alpha+1}\,d\vec{x}=0.

(3.9)

Thus we have

\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}g\nabla_{h}Z\cdot v\,d\vec{x}=\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{g}{\alpha+2}\int\bigl[(Z^{\alpha+2}-1)-\dfrac{\alpha+2}{\alpha+1}(Z^{\alpha+1}-1)\bigr]\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}.

(3.10)

Moreover, observe that when
|Z-1|
is small enough, one has

\displaystyle\dfrac{\alpha+2}{4(\alpha+1)^{2}}\bigl|Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr|^{2}\leq(Z^{\alpha+2}-1)-\dfrac{\alpha+2}{\alpha+1}(Z^{\alpha+1}-1)

(3.11)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\alpha+2}{(\alpha+1)^{2}}\bigl|Z^{\alpha+1}-1\bigr|^{2}

Therefore from (
3.7
) and (
3.10
), we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{\alpha+2}\int\bigl[(Z^{\alpha+2}-1)-\dfrac{\alpha+2}{\alpha+1}(Z^{\alpha+1}-1)\bigr]\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}

(3.12)

\displaystyle+\int Z\bigl(\mu\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr|^{2}+\mu\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}=0.

Integrating (
3.9
) and (
3.12
) with respect to time yields the conservations of mass and energy.
Moreover, from (
1.17
), we have

\displaystyle\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},{}

\displaystyle\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{p}}\lesssim\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha/2}v}\bigr|_{H^{1}}\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|_{H^{1}}+\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v}\bigr|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha/2}v}\bigr|_{H^{1}}\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|_{H^{1}}+\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v}\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2/p}\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v}\bigr|_{H^{1}}^{1-2/p}

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert{z^{\alpha/2}v}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}+\bigl\Arrowvert{z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2/p}\bigl\Arrowvert{z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}^{1-2/p},

(3.13)

for
2<p<\infty
. Taking
p=3
and integrating the corresponding quantities with respect to time will complete the proof of the proposition.
∎

Next lemma is concerning the estimate for
\nabla v
:

Proposition 5
.

Under the same assumptions as in Proposition
4
, one has that

\displaystyle\int\bigl[\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu+\lambda}{2}\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|^{2}\bigr]\,d\vec{x}

(3.14)

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\alpha+1}\int gz^{\alpha}(Z^{\alpha+1}-1)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\vec{x}+\int_{0}^{T}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{0}\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu+\lambda}{2}\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{0}\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\partial_{z}v_{0}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\alpha+1}\int gz^{\alpha}(Z_{0}^{\alpha+1}-1)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{0}\,d\vec{x}+C\int_{0}^{T}\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt+C\int_{0}^{T}\bigl((\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}+1)\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigr)\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr)\,dt,

for some constant
0<C<\infty
.

Proof.

Taking the
L^{2}
-inner product of
\eqref{FB-CPE}_{2}
with
v_{t}
implies, after applying integrations by parts,

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\int\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu+\lambda}{2}\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}+\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}

(3.15)

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\alpha+1}\int gz^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}\,d\vec{x}=-\int\biggl(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}v\cdot\nabla_{h}v\cdot v_{t}

\displaystyle-\mu\nabla_{h}\log Z\cdot\nabla_{h}v\cdot v_{t}-(\mu+\lambda)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\nabla_{h}\log Z\cdot v_{t}\biggr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle-\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}W\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

Also, one has

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\alpha+1}\int gz^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}\,d\vec{x}=\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{-\dfrac{1}{\alpha+1}\int gz^{\alpha}(Z^{\alpha+1}-1)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}

\displaystyle~~~~+\underbrace{\int gz^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}Z_{t}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\vec{x}}_{-I_{3}}.

We estimate
I_{2}
first. After substituting (
1.18
), one has

\displaystyle I_{2}=-\int z^{\alpha+1}Z^{\alpha-1}\bigl((\alpha+1)\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z+\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}Z\bigr)\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~+\int\biggl(Z^{\alpha-1}\bigl((\alpha+1)\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v\,d\xi\cdot\nabla_{h}Z+\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\xi Z\bigr)\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\biggr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~=:I_{2}^{\prime}+I_{2}^{\prime\prime}.

Notice that
Z,\overline{z^{\alpha}v},\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}
are independent of the
z
-variable. Therefore after applying Hölder’s, Minkowski’s, the Sobolev embedding inequalities, and (
2.1
), one has that

\displaystyle I_{2}^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}(\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\bigr|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|_{L^{4}}+\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha}\nabla_{h}v}\bigr|_{L^{4}})\bigl|z^{\alpha/2+1}\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl|z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\,dz

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{1/2}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha}\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}})

\displaystyle~~~~~~\times\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|z^{\alpha/2}\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\bigl|z^{\alpha/2}\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\bigl|z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\,dz\lesssim\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle~~~~~~+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigr)\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle I_{2}^{\prime\prime}\leq\int_{0}^{1}\bigl[\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|_{L^{4}}\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\bigl|v\bigr|_{\infty}\,d\xi+\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|_{L^{4}}\,d\xi\bigr]\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\,dz

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl(\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|_{L^{4}}(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha/2}\bigl|v\bigr|_{\infty}^{2}\,d\xi)^{1/2}+(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha/2}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,d\xi)^{1/4}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\times(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha/2}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|_{H^{1}}^{2}\,d\xi)^{1/4}\bigr)z^{\alpha/2+1/2}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\,dz

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}+1)\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}.

Therefore, we arrive at the inequality

I_{2}\lesssim\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}+1\bigr)\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}.

On the other hand, employing similar arguments, one can obtain the following estimates for
I_{1},I_{3}
:

\displaystyle I_{1}\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{3}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{6}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{6}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{3}}\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle~~~~+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr),

\displaystyle I_{3}\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},

where we have applied, above, Hardy’s inequality as follows:

\displaystyle\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dz\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}z^{2}(\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl|\partial_{z}v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2})\,dz\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}z^{4}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(3.16)

\displaystyle+\bigl|\partial_{z}v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dz\underbrace{\lesssim\cdots\lesssim}_{\max\{[\alpha/2]-1,0\}\text{ times}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Therefore, from (
3.15
), after summing up the inequalities above, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\int\bigl[\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu+\lambda}{2}\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\bigr|^{2}+\dfrac{\mu}{2}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|^{2}\bigr]\,d\vec{x}

(3.17)

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\alpha+1}\int gz^{\alpha}(Z^{\alpha+1}-1)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}+\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl((\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}+1)\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigr)\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr).

Integrating the above inequality with respect to the time variable will conclude the proof of the proposition.
∎

3.2
The temporal and horizontal derivative estimates

This section includes the temporal derivative estimate and the horizontal (tangential) derivative estimates.

After applying
\partial_{t}
to
\eqref{FB-CPE}_{2}
, (
1.17
) and (
1.18
), the following equations hold

\begin{cases}z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}(\partial_{t}v_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{h}v_{t}+W\partial_{z}v_{t})+z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}(v_{t}\cdot\nabla_{h}v+W_{t}\partial_{z}v)\\
~~~~~~~~+\alpha z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha-1}Z_{t}(v_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{h}v+W\partial_{z}v)+g(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}\nabla_{h}Z)_{t}\\
~~~~=\mu\Delta_{h}v_{t}+(\mu+\lambda)\nabla_{h}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}+\mu\partial_{zz}v_{t}+\mu\nabla_{h}\log Z\cdot\nabla_{h}v_{t}\\
~~~~~~~~+(\mu+\lambda)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}\nabla_{h}\log Z+\mu\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{t}\cdot\nabla_{h}v\\
~~~~~~~~+(\mu+\lambda)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{t},\\
\partial_{t}Z_{t}+(\alpha+1)\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z_{t}+\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}Z_{t}+(\alpha+1)\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{t}}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\\
~~~~~~~~+\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}}Z=0,\\
z^{\alpha}W_{t}=z^{\alpha+1}\bigl((\alpha+1)\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\cdot\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{t}+(\alpha+1)\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{t}}\cdot\nabla_{h}\log Z\\
~~~~~~~~+\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}}\bigr)-(\alpha+1)\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v\,d\xi\cdot\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{t}\\
~~~~~~~~-(\alpha+1)\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v_{t}\,d\xi\cdot\nabla_{h}\log Z-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}\,d\xi,\\
\partial_{z}Z_{t}=0.\end{cases}

(3.18)

Now we derive the temporal derivative estimate:

Proposition 6
.

Under the same assumptions as in Proposition
4
, one has that

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}

(3.19)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int Z\bigl(\mu\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr|^{2}+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}\bigr|^{2}+\mu\bigl|\partial_{z}v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z_{0}^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v_{1}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{2}\int Z_{0}^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{1}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{H}(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}},\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}\bigr)+C\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{3}}^{3}\,dt,

for some constant
0<C<\infty
. Recall that
\mathcal{H}(\cdot)
is a polynomial of its arguments with
\mathcal{H}(0)=0
.

Proof.

Taking the
L^{2}
-inner product of
\eqref{eq:dt-FBCPE}_{1}
with
Zv_{t}
yields, after applying integration by parts,

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}+\int g(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}\nabla_{h}Z)_{t}\cdot Zv_{t}\,d\vec{x}

(3.20)

\displaystyle+\int Z\bigl(\mu\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr|^{2}+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}\bigr|^{2}+\mu\bigl|\partial_{z}v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=\int\mu Z\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{t}\cdot\nabla_{h}v\cdot v_{t}+(\mu+\lambda)Zv_{t}\cdot\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{t}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle-\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}v_{t}\cdot\nabla_{h}v\cdot v_{t}+\alpha z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}Z_{t}(v_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{h}v)\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle-\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}W_{t}\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}-\alpha\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}Z_{t}W\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=:I_{4}+I_{5}+I_{6}+I_{7}.

At the same time, after substituting
\eqref{eq:dt-FBCPE}_{2}
and noticing that
Z
is independent of the
z
-variable, one has,

\displaystyle\int g(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}\nabla_{h}Z)_{t}\cdot Zv_{t}\,d\vec{x}=\dfrac{g}{\alpha+1}\int z^{\alpha}(\nabla_{h}Z^{\alpha+1})_{t}\cdot Zv_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=-g\int Z^{\alpha+1}Z_{t}\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}}+Z^{\alpha}Z_{t}\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{t}}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=g\int Z^{\alpha}Z_{t}\bigl(\partial_{t}Z_{t}+(\alpha+1)\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z_{t}+\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}Z_{t}+\alpha\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{t}}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\bigr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}-\dfrac{\alpha g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha-1}Z_{t}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~+\alpha g\int Z^{\alpha}Z_{t}\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{t}}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{(1-\alpha)g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha}\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~-\dfrac{(\alpha+1)\alpha g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha-1}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\,d\vec{x}=:\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}

\displaystyle~~~~-I_{8}.

Similarly as before, we estimate the terms involving the vertical velocity
W
first. In fact, we have from
\eqref{eq:dt-FBCPE}_{3}
that

\displaystyle I_{6}=-(\alpha+1)\int Z^{\alpha+1}(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}v}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v\,d\xi)\cdot\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{t}\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~-(\alpha+1)\int Z^{\alpha+1}(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{t}}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v_{t}\,d\xi)\cdot\nabla_{h}\log Z\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~-\int Z^{\alpha+1}(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}\,d\xi)\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=:I_{6}^{\prime}+I_{6}^{\prime\prime}+I_{6}^{\prime\prime\prime}.

Applying integration by parts yields

\displaystyle I_{6}^{\prime}=(\alpha+1)\int Z^{\alpha+1}(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\xi)(\log Z)_{t}\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~+(\alpha+1)^{2}\int Z^{\alpha}(\log Z)_{t}(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}v}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v\,d\xi)\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~+(\alpha+1)\int Z^{\alpha+1}(\log Z)_{t}(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}v}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v\,d\xi)\cdot\nabla_{h}(\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t})\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=:I_{6,1}^{\prime}+I_{6,2}^{\prime}+I_{6,3}^{\prime}.

Now we apply Hölder’s, Minkowski’s, and the Sobolev embedding inequalities,

\displaystyle I_{6,1}^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{8}}z^{\alpha/2}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl(\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha}\nabla_{h}v}\bigr|_{L^{8}}+(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|_{L^{8}}^{2}\,d\xi)^{1/2}\bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{H^{1}}\bigl|z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\bigl|z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr|_{H^{1}}^{1/2}\,dz\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert{z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr),

\displaystyle I_{6,2}^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|_{L^{16}}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{8}}z^{\alpha/2}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl(\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\bigr|_{L^{16}}

\displaystyle~~~~+(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\bigl|v\bigr|_{L^{16}}\,d\xi)^{1/2}\bigr)\,dz\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{H^{1}}\bigl|z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr|_{H^{1}}\,dz\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr),

\displaystyle I_{6,3}^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\bigl(\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{\infty}}z^{\alpha/2}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}+\bigl|\nabla_{h}\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{4}}z^{\alpha/2}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigr)

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl(\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\bigr|_{L^{\infty}}+(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\bigl|v\bigr|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\,d\xi)^{1/2}\bigr)\,dz\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{H^{2}}\bigl|z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr|_{H^{1}}\,dz

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle~~~~+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr).

Similarly,

\displaystyle I_{6}^{\prime\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{4}}z^{\alpha/2}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl(\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{t}}\bigr|_{L^{4}}+(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{4}}^{2}\,d\xi)^{1/2}\bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr),

\displaystyle I_{6}^{\prime\prime\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{\infty}}z^{\alpha/2}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\bigl(\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha}\nabla_{h}v_{t}}\bigr|_{L^{2}}+(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,d\xi)^{1/2}\,dz

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}.

At the same time, from (
1.18
), we have

\displaystyle I_{7}=-\alpha(\alpha+1)\int Z^{\alpha-1}Z_{t}\bigl(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}v}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v\,d\xi\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~-\alpha\int Z^{\alpha}Z_{t}\bigl(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\xi\bigr)\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{t}\,d\vec{x}=:I_{7}^{\prime}+I_{7}^{\prime\prime},

with

\displaystyle I_{7}^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|_{L^{8}}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{8}}z^{\alpha/2}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl(\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\bigr|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle~~~~+(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\bigl|v\bigr|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\,d\xi)^{1/2}\bigr)\,dz\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr),

\displaystyle I_{7}^{\prime\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\bigl|\partial_{z}v\bigr|_{L^{8}}z^{\alpha/2}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl(\bigl|\overline{z^{\alpha}\nabla_{h}v}\bigr|_{L^{8}}

\displaystyle~~~~+(\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|_{L^{8}}^{2}\,d\xi)^{1/2}\bigr)\,dz\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle I_{6}+I_{7}\lesssim\mathcal{H}(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}})\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr).

We list the estimates for the rest in the following:

\displaystyle I_{4}=-\int\mu(\log Z)_{t}\nabla_{h}Z\cdot\nabla_{h}v\cdot v_{t}+(\mu+\lambda)(\log Z)_{t}v_{t}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~-\int\mu Z(\log Z)_{t}\Delta_{h}v\cdot v_{t}+(\mu+\lambda)Z(\log Z)_{t}v_{t}\cdot\nabla_{h}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~-\int\mu Z(\log Z)_{t}\nabla_{h}v:\nabla_{h}v_{t}+(\mu+\lambda)Z(\log Z)_{t}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\bigl(\bigl|\nabla_{h}Z\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|_{L^{8}}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{8}}+\bigl|\nabla_{h}^{2}v\bigr|_{L^{4}}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle~~~~+\bigl|\nabla_{h}v\bigr|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr|_{L^{2}}\bigr)\,dz\lesssim\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}+1\bigr)\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigr),

\displaystyle I_{5}\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{3}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{6}}+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{3}}\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{6}}

\displaystyle~~~~+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{6}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{6}}\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{6}}\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{1/2}+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{3/2}+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl\Arrowvert v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}\lesssim\mathcal{H}(\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}})\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}\bigr),

\displaystyle I_{8}\lesssim\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{3}}^{3}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{3/2}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{3/2}+\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}_{h}}^{3}

\displaystyle~~~~+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{3}\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}^{3},

where we have applied the inequality (
3.16
).
After summing up the inequalities above from (
3.20
), one has the following inequality:

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{t}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}

(3.21)

\displaystyle+\int Z\bigl(\mu\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{t}\bigr|^{2}+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{t}\bigr|^{2}+\mu\bigl|\partial_{z}v_{t}\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{H}(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}},\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}\bigr)+\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{3}}^{3}.

Integrating this inequality in the temporal variable will finish the proof.
∎

~~~~~~~~+\alpha z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha-1}Z_{h}(v_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{h}v+W\partial_{z}v)+g(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}\nabla_{h}Z)_{h}\\

(3.22)

Proposition 7
.

(3.24)

Recall that
\mathcal{H}(\cdot)
is a polynomial of its arguments with

.

Proof.

After taking inner product of
\eqref{eq:dh-FBCPE}_{1}
with
Zv_{h}
, one has after applying integration by parts in the resultant,

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v_{h}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{h}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}

(3.25)

\displaystyle+\int\biggl(Z\bigl(\mu\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{h}\bigr|^{2}+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{h}\bigr|^{2}+\mu\bigl|\partial_{z}v_{h}\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\biggr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=\int\biggl(\mu Z\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}v\cdot v_{h}+(\mu+\lambda)Zv_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{h}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\biggr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle-\int\biggl(z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}v_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}v\cdot v_{h}+\alpha z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}Z_{h}(v_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{h}v)\cdot v_{h}\biggr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle-\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}W_{h}\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{h}\,d\vec{x}-\alpha\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}Z_{h}W\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{h}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{\alpha g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha-1}Z_{t}\bigl|Z_{h}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}-\alpha g\int Z^{\alpha}Z_{h}\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{h}}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle-\dfrac{(1-\alpha)g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{h}\bigr|^{2}\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{(\alpha+1)\alpha g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha-1}\bigl|Z_{h}\bigr|^{2}\overline{z^{\alpha}v}\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\,d\vec{x}=:I_{9}+I_{10}+I_{11}

\displaystyle+I_{12}+I_{13}+I_{14}+I_{15}+I_{16}.

As before, we shall estimate the integrals involving the vertical velocity first.
Substituting (
1.18
) and
\eqref{eq:dh-FBCPE}_{3}
yields

\displaystyle I_{11}=-(\alpha+1)\int Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}v}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v\,d\xi\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{h}\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{h}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~-(\alpha+1)\int Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}v_{h}}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v_{h}\,d\xi\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{h}\log Z\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{h}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~-\int Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{h}}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{h}\,d\xi\bigr)\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{h}\,d\vec{x},

\displaystyle I_{12}=-\alpha(\alpha+1)\int Z^{\alpha-1}Z_{h}\bigl(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}v}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}v\,d\xi\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{h}Z\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{h}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle~~~~-\alpha\int Z^{\alpha}Z_{h}\bigl(z^{\alpha+1}\overline{z^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v}-\int_{0}^{z}\xi^{\alpha}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\,d\xi\bigr)\partial_{z}v\cdot v_{h}\,d\vec{x}.

Then after replacing the subscript

with

, the estimates for

are the same as those for

and

, above. Thus we have

I_{11}+I_{12}\lesssim\mathcal{H}(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}})\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}\bigl\Arrowvert v_{h}\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}.

The rest of the terms can be handled in the same way. That is, it holds that

\displaystyle I_{9}+I_{10}+I_{13}+I_{14}+I_{15}+I_{16}

\displaystyle~~~~\lesssim\mathcal{H}(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}},\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle~~~~\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}\bigr).

Summing up the inequalities above yields

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v_{h}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}+\dfrac{g}{2}\int Z^{\alpha}\bigl|Z_{h}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}

(3.26)

\displaystyle+\int Z\bigl(\mu\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{h}\bigr|^{2}+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{h}\bigr|^{2}+\mu\bigl|\partial_{z}v_{h}\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{H}(\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},\bigl\Arrowvert Z_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}},\bigl\Arrowvert v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}},\bigl\Arrowvert z^{\alpha/2}v_{t}\bigr\Arrowvert_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\times\bigl(\bigl\Arrowvert\partial_{z}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{2}_{h}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}v\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}+\bigl\Arrowvert\nabla_{h}Z\bigr\Arrowvert_{H^{1}}^{2}\bigr).

Integrating this inequality in the temporal variable completes the proof of (
3.23
).

In order to show (
3.24
), we take inner product of
\eqref{eq:dh-FBCPE}_{1}
with
-(Zv_{hh})_{h}
. After applying integration by parts, it follows,

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{dt}\biggl\{\dfrac{1}{2}\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}\bigl|v_{hh}\bigr|^{2}\,d\vec{x}\biggr\}-g\int z^{\alpha}Z_{hh}\mathrm{div}_{h}\,(Zv_{hh})\,d\vec{x}

(3.27)

\displaystyle+\int Z\bigl(\mu\bigl|\nabla_{h}v_{hh}\bigr|^{2}+(\mu+\lambda)\bigl|\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{hh}\bigr|^{2}+\mu\bigl|\partial_{z}v_{hh}\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle=\int\bigl(\mu\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}v_{h}+(\mu+\lambda)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v_{h}\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{h}\bigr)\cdot Zv_{hh}

\displaystyle-\bigl(\mu\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}v+(\mu+\lambda)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,v\nabla_{h}(\log Z)_{h}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(Zv_{hh}\bigr)_{h}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle+\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha-1}\bigl(Zv_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}v+\alpha Z_{h}(v_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{h}v)\bigr)(Zv_{hh})_{h}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle+\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}W_{h}\partial_{z}v\cdot(Zv_{hh})_{h}\,d\vec{x}+\alpha\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha-1}Z_{h}W\partial_{z}v\cdot(Zv_{hh})_{h}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle-\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}\bigl(Zv_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}v_{h}+\alpha Z_{h}(\partial_{t}v_{h}+v\cdot\nabla_{h}v_{h})\bigr)\cdot v_{hh}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle-\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha+1}W_{h}\partial_{z}v_{h}\cdot v_{hh}\,d\vec{x}-\alpha\int z^{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}Z_{h}W\partial_{z}v_{h}\cdot v_{hh}\,d\vec{x}

\displaystyle+g\int z^{\alpha}\bigl((Z^{\alpha}-1)Z_{hh}+\alpha Z^{\alpha-1}\bigl|Z_{h}\bigr|^{2}\bigr)\mathrm{div}_{h}\,(Zv_{hh})\,d\vec{x}
